So basket rim: image-left edge (min y) at y=0.169 (col 710); assuming the rim spans symmetric, rim center y = 0.169 + halfwidth. The front rim pixel (555,820) gave y=0.2476 — but for a point at the middle of the front rim, its y should equal the center y (0.169 + w/2)? No! For an image-axis-aligned rectangle, the middle of the front edge is at y = center y. So y_center ≈ 0.2476. And the rim half-width ≈ 0.2476-0.169 = 0.079, so rim spans y from 0.169 to 0.326.

x: front rim point x=0.0717 (z=0.0954, slightly below the rim top since we're at the outer face); back rim at x=? occluded. The rim depth (x-extent) ~0.15, so back rim x ≈ 0.072-0.15 = -0.08, center x ≈ -0.004.

Hmm, but a concern with the "In" predicate: the predicate typically requires the object's body center to be within the basket volume. If the box lands on top of the liner fold (the liner's inner rim), it might be perched. Looking at the agentview at step 8, the liner is quite bulgy. The interior opening looks reasonably wide.
